02-03.01_调试基础

每位程序员，无论经验多少，都会遇到错误看似无法解决而感到沮丧的时刻。

即便是传奇人物，计算机领域的先驱格蕾丝·赫柏，也曾面临过这样的挑战。

1947年，在哈佛马克二型计算机上工作时，赫柏和她的团队发现一只飞蛾被困在继电器中，导致系统故障，这一发现催生了“调试”这个术语。

这个故事不仅仅是一个历史轶事。

它象征着调试这一普遍且永恒的斗争，而调试正是将高效程序员与其他程序员区分开来的基本技能。

在本视频中，我们将解释调试的基本原理。

调试是识别并解决计算机程序中错误或缺陷的过程。

要记住的是，没有代码是完全没有错误的。

错误是编程中很自然的一部分，即使是最有经验的开发人员也会经常遇到。

让我们先来看看你可能遇到的第一类错误：语法错误。

回顾一下，语法错误是指代码中违反了编程语言规则的错误。

当你编写的代码不符合编程语言的语法规则时，就会出现语法错误。

这些错误通常会被编译器或解释器捕获。

既然我们已经定义了语法错误，让我们看一个C#中可能发生语法错误的例子。

假设你有一个名为Start的方法。

并且假设你有一个名为welcome Message的字符串，初始值为“welcome to my web application”。

但是你忘记正确地闭合字符串，也就是没有在字符串末尾添加结束引号。

当你尝试编译这段代码时，编译器会给出类似这样的错误信息。

错误CS1002，应输入分号。

错误CS1513，应输入右大括号。

这是因为编译器期望通过写入结束引号和右大括号来闭合开头的引号。

编译器会感到困惑，因此这段代码需要被调试。

幸运的是，语法错误是你最不用担心的问题之一，因为它们通常会被编译器捕获。

现代代码编辑器也通常会提供如何修复这些错误的建议。

Visual Studio Code甚至会在你尝试编译代码之前，就提供发现语法错误的行号，并在它认为导致错误的代码下方画上红色波浪线。

此外，大多数编辑器现在都使用颜色编码，这有助于你直观地发现错误。

当有错误时，代码的显示效果往往会显得不正常。

随着时间和练习的积累，你甚至会在点击编译之前就开始识别出这些错误。

讲完语法错误，现在我们来看看运行时错误，处理这类错误需要更多的知识和经验。

与发生在编译阶段的语法错误不同，运行时错误发生在程序执行期间。

它们通常会导致程序崩溃或行为异常。

出乎意料。

当程序运行时遇到无法执行的操作，就会发生运行时错误。

这些通常是由无效操作或内存问题引起的。

为了说明这一点，让我们考虑一个例子，其中除法操作如果处理不当会导致运行时错误。

这里我们有一个名为Divide Numbers的方法，它接收两个参数：分子和分母。

问题出现是因为程序员必须使用基本的除法运算。

如果代码在没有适当错误处理的情况下尝试执行除法，用户可能会尝试除以零，我们都知道这是不可能的，这将导致错误。

这类错误通常由运行时环境（如.NET框架）来管理。

但是，这些环境在处理这些错误时并不总是有效的。

它们常常会导致程序突然崩溃。

因此，虽然运行时错误可能令人沮丧，但它们通常很明显，因为程序会失败，从而提醒你注意问题。

真正的挑战在于逻辑错误，那些不会导致崩溃，但会悄悄地产生错误结果的隐藏错误。

让我们来看看逻辑错误。

回顾一下，这些是程序逻辑中的错误，会产生不正确的结果，但不会使程序崩溃。

逻辑错误通常最难发现，因为代码语法是正确的。

所以编译器可以编译通过，没有崩溃，代码看似可以运行，但它产生了错误的结果。

让我们来看一个例子。

这里我们有一个名为Apply Discount的方法。

它有两个参数：价格和折扣百分比。

假设我们犯了一个逻辑错误，错误地使用了减法而不是乘法来计算折扣。

代码将编译通过，没有语法错误，也不会产生任何运行时错误。

然而，它是错误的，因为它没有产生正确的结果。

用1000美元和百分之五十的折扣调用这个方法，应该得到850美元，但由于程序员错误地使用了带减法的公式，它会得到985美元，这是一个错误。

既然我们已经确定了不同类型的错误，让我们探讨如何使用调试技术来诊断和修复它们。

调试工具箱中的第一个工具是断点。

回顾一下，断点允许你在特定位置暂停代码的执行，以便检查程序的状态。

在代码中设置断点可以帮助你在关键点暂停执行，并在程序的不同阶段检查变量值和程序流程。

要在像Visual Studio Code这样的IDE中使用断点，你可以将断点设置在认为有情况发生或者想要更深入检查情况的那一行代码上。

请记住，断点对语法错误无效，因为它们是在程序运行时才停止或中断执行。

因此，断点适用于运行时错误和逻辑错误。

在关键点设置断点暂停代码后，下一步是检查变量的值。

检查变量值允许你查看代码中不同位置的变量的当前值。

检查完变量值后，你可以逐步执行代码。

逐步执行代码允许你一次一行地执行程序，以便仔细观察其行为。

一行一行地逐步执行代码有助于你理解程序的流程以及可能出错的地方。

但有时你需要代码运行并打印消息，以便你检查其内容。

传统的做法是将错误消息打印到输出中，这样你就可以进行分析。

你是否曾在应用中遇到过这样的情况：弹出一个窗口，告诉你“哎呀，出错了”，并附带一堆用户通常看不懂的技术信息？

嗯，这是因为程序员加入了某种消息打印功能。

最基本的实现方式是在控制台中插入“控制台点写入行”语句进行打印。

如果你想同时检查多个变量，或者只是想通过一次打印来查看代码流程，这会很有用。

阅读和理解错误消息可以告诉你代码失败的位置和原因。

打印消息时，要密切关注错误消息中的行号和描述，以便快速定位和修复代码中的问题。

虽然像阅读错误消息这样的传统方法很有价值，但在现代IDE中还有一种更实用的方法：调试器的检查器。

在本视频中，我们探讨了调试的基本原理。

通过了解常见的错误类型，如语法错误、运行时错误和逻辑错误，你可以更好地预见和处理代码中出现的问题。

随着你不断练习和应用这些技巧，你将获得应对任何编码挑战所需的信心和技能。

英文原文

Every programmer,regardless of experience,encounters moments of frustration when errors seem impossible to solve.

Even the legendary Grace Hopper,a pioneer in computing,faced such challenges.

In 1947,while working on the Harvard Mark II computer,Hopper and her team discovered a moth trapped in a relay causing the system to malfunction,a discovery that led to the coining of the term debugging.

This story is more than just a historical anecdote.

It symbolizes the universal and timeless struggle of debugging,an essential skill that separates effective programmers from the rest.

In this video,we will explain the basic principles of debugging.

Debugging is the process of identifying and resolving errors or bugs in a computer program.

The thing to remember is that no code is ever completely error-free.

Errors are a natural part of programming,and even the most experienced developers encounter them regularly.

Let's start by looking at the first type of error you might encounter,syntax errors.

Recall that syntax errors are mistakes in the code that violate the rules of the programming language.

Syntax errors occur when you write code that doesn't follow the syntax rule of the programming language.

These are usually caught by the compiler or interpreter.

Now that we've defined syntax errors,let's look at an example in C#where a syntax error could arise.

Suppose you have a method called start.

And let's suppose you have a string called welcome Message with an initial value of welcome to my web application.

But you've forgotten to close the string properly by failing to add the final quotes in the string.

When you try to compile this code,the compiler will give you an error similar to this.

Error CS1002,semicolon expected.

Error CS1513 bracket expected.

This is because the compiler was expecting the initial quotes to be closed by writing the closing quotes and the closing brackets too.

The compiler will be confused and thus the code needs to be debugged.

Fortunately,syntax errors are among the least of your concerns as they typically are caught by the compiler.

Modern code editors also often provide suggestions on how to fix them.

Visual Studio Code will even provide a line number where the syntax error was found and put a red line under the code it thinks caused the error,even before you try to compile the code.

Additionally,most editors now use color coding,which helps you spot errors visually.

Something will often look off when there's a mistake.

With time and practice,you'll start recognizing these errors even before you hit compile.

Now that we've covered syntax errors,let's move on to runtime errors,which require a bit more knowledge and expertise to handle.

Unlike syntax errors,which occurred during the compilation phase,runtime errors occurred during the program's execution.

They often cause the program to crash or behave on.

Unexpectedly.

Runtime errors happen when the program runs and it encounters an operation it cannot handle.

These are typically caused by invalid operations or memory issues.

To illustrate this,let's consider an example where a division operation can lead to a runtime error if not properly handled.

Here we have a method called divide Numbers that receives two parameters,the numerator and the denominator.

The issue arises because the programmer has to use a basic division operation.

If the code attempts to divide two numbers without proper error handling,a user might try to divide by zero,which as we know is impossible,and this will result in an error.

This type of error is usually managed by the runtime environment such as the.NET framework.

However,these environments aren't always effective at handling these errors.

They often cause the program to crash abruptly.

So while runtime errors can be frustrating,they're usually obvious because the program fails,alerting you to the issue.

The real challenge lies in the logical errors,those hidden mistakes that don't cause a crash but silently produce incorrect results.

Let's examine logical errors.

Recall that these are mistakes in the program's logic that produces incorrect results but do not crash the program.

Logical errors are often the hardest to spot because the code syntax is correct.

So the compiler compiles,nothing crashes,and the code appears to work,but it produces incorrect results.

Let's look at an example.

Here we have a method called applied discount.

It has two parameters,the price and the discount percentage.

Suppose we make a logical error by incorrectly applying a discount percentage using subtraction instead of multiplication.

The code will compile without syntax errors and it will not produce any runtime errors.

However,it is incorrect because it doesn't produce the right results.

Invoking this method with$1,000 and a 50%discount should give you$850,but since the programmer wrongly used a formula with a subtraction,it will give$985,which is an error.

Now that we've identified the different types of errors,let's explore how to diagnose and fix them with debugging techniques.

The first tool in your debugging toolkit is Breakpoints.

Recall that breakpoints allow you to pause the execution of your code at specific points so you can inspect the state of the program.

Setting breakpoints in your code can help you pause execution at critical points and examine variable values in program flow at different stages of your program.

To use breakpoints in an IDE like Visual Studio Code,you set them in the line where you think something is happening or where you want to inspect what is happening more deeply.

Remember that breakpoints do not work for syntax errors since they stop or break the execution when the program is running.

Thus breakpoints work for runtime and logical errors.

After setting your breakpoints in pausing your code at critical points,the next step is to inspect the values of your variables.

Inspecting variable values allows you to check the current values of variables at different points in your code.

Once you have checked the values of the variables,you can step through the code.

Stepping through your code allows you to execute your program one line at a time to closely observe its behavior.

Stepping through your code line by line helps you understand how the program flows and where it might go wrong.

But sometimes you need the code to run and print messages allowing you to examine their content.

The classical way to do this is by printing error messages to the output so you can analyze them.

Have you ever encountered an error in an app where the window comes up and tells you,oops,something failed,with a bunch of technical information that users often do not understand?

Well,that's there because a programmer included a message printing of some sort.

The most basic way to do this is to insert console.write Line statements to print in a console.

This can be useful if you want to inspect many variables simultaneously or just see the code flow in a single printing.

Reading and understanding error messages can tell you where and why your code fails.

When printing messages,pay close attention to the line numbers and descriptions in error messages to quickly locate and fix issues in your code.

While traditional methods like reading error messages are valuable,there is an even more practical approach available in modern IDEs,the debugger's inspector.

In this video,we have explored the basic principles of debugging.

By understanding the common types of errors,such as syntax,runtime,and logical,you can better anticipate and address the issues that arise in your code.

As you continue to practice and apply these techniques,you'll gain the confidence and skills needed to tackle any coding challenge that comes your way.

02-03.02_实践调试

调试是每位开发者都应掌握的宝贵技能，它能确保你的代码顺畅、高效地运行。

代码中出现错误是不可避免的，但知道如何识别并修复它们，可以节省时间并防止潜在问题。

在本视频中，我们将学习应用调试技术来识别和修复代码中的错误。

让我们来探索一些可以辅助你调试工作的工具。

对许多开发者来说，首选工具之一是Visual Studio Code。

这是一个常见的场景：一段简单的、用来执行两个数相除的代码。

在编译器看来，一切似乎都是正确的。

变量名清晰，逗号和分号也都放在了正确的位置。

因此，编译器没有标记任何语法错误，这意味着代码结构或格式没有问题。

但是，如果这段代码运行起来，并且用户传递了零作为分母参数，就会发生运行时错误。

你只有在程序执行时才会发现运行时错误。

在这种情况下会发生错误，是因为分母为零在数学上是不成立的。

为了解决这个问题，许多程序员会使用调试器。

调试器是一种帮助开发者查找并修复代码中错误或缺陷的工具。

它允许你逐步运行代码、检查变量并了解执行流程，从而找出问题所在。

前端开发者最常用的调试器之一，就集成在Visual Studio Code（简称VS Code）中，你可以通过右上角的调试器按钮来访问它。

在VS Code调试器中，你可以设置断点来暂停程序执行，监视变量，并逐步执行代码以准确了解其运行方式。

这使得VS Code成为一个强大的工具，可以快速识别和修复错误，从而提高代码的质量和可靠性。

让我们看看在我们这个两数相除的示例中，这是如何工作的。

我们构建了“除法数字”方法。

它接收两个参数，并返回它们的商。

在这个示例中，我们将执行这段代码，并以零作为分母调用该方法。

为此，我将结果存储在一个名为result的变量中。

这里我们是用分子10除以分母0，然后打印结果。

代码编译通过了，所以没有任何语法错误。

但是当我运行程序时，它显示“结果是”，后面跟着一个无穷大符号。

这就是一个运行时错误。

当遇到这样的错误时，你可以依靠调试器的几个功能来帮助你调试代码。

第一个是断点。

这是“除法数字”方法的代码。

在VS Code中，你可以通过点击行号左侧的装订线区域来设置断点，程序执行到该行时会暂停。

我们可以在这一行添加一个断点。

下次程序运行到这一行时，它会在执行前停下来，这样你就可以判断在这个断点之前可能发生了什么。

通常，你会在变量值发生变化的行上添加断点，这样之后就可以检查它们的值，看看是否符合预期。

设置好断点后，下一步就是检查变量。

这时，你就可以准确地确定当程序运行到断点时，代码中究竟发生了什么。

在执行期间检查变量的值，有助于你验证程序是否按预期处理数据。

调试器包含一个叫做“检查器”的工具。

你可以用它悬停在变量上，来显示它当前持有的值。

在这个例子中，分母是零。

检查完变量后，你可以使用调试器中的“逐过程”功能，手动进入下一行代码。

这让你可以使用工具栏上的控件，一行一行地浏览代码，以精确跟踪程序的执行流程，并捕捉可能出现问题的地方。

调试器工具栏显示了一组可供你使用的按钮。

第一个按钮是“继续”，它会恢复程序执行，直到遇到下一个断点或程序结束。

下一个按钮是“逐过程”，它执行当前行代码并移动到下一行，但不会进入该行调用的函数或方法内部。

然后是“逐语句”按钮。

这会执行当前行代码，如果该行包含函数或方法，则会进入该函数内部，以便在其中进行逐行调试。

接下来是“跳出”按钮，它会完成当前函数的执行，并返回到调用该函数的代码处，然后暂停。

然后是“重启”按钮，它会停止当前调试会话，并从头开始重新启动。

最后，“停止”按钮会完全结束当前的调试会话，停止所有代码执行。

一旦调试器根据断点要求暂停了执行，就可以开始逐过程调试了。

在这个例子中，我添加了一个名为result的变量，因为我想检查操作的结果。

为此，我在这一行添加了一个断点。

此时，你可以检查变量，并查看代码内部发生了什么。

如果你愿意，可以逐步执行，以准确找到错误发生的位置。

在这个例子中，我们注意到变量result的值是零，这就会导致我们正在排查的错误。

逐过程调试代码让你能够精确定位问题发生的位置。

例如，当我逐过程执行这一行，然后检查变量时，我得到了一个奇怪的结果，它的值是无穷大，这正是最初出现的意外行为。

有了正确的关注点，你就能定位代码中的错误。

使用调试器的检查器，可以帮助你了解变量的值，从而获取更多关于它们的信息。

在本视频中，我们探讨了调试技术在高效识别和修复代码错误方面的重要性。

我们使用了Visual Studio Code调试器，它允许你设置断点、检查变量并逐步执行代码，为理解和解决问题提供了强大的途径。

通过掌握这些工具和技术，你将增强自己解决问题的能力。

并成为一名更有能力、更高效的程序员。

Debugging is a valuable skill for every developer,ensuring that your code runs smoothly and efficiently.

Mistakes in code are inevitable,but knowing how to identify and fix them can save time and prevent potential issues.

In this video,we will learn to apply debugging techniques to identify and fix errors in code.

Let's explore some tools that can aid your debugging efforts.

One of the go-to tools for many developers is Visual Studio Code.

Here's a common scenario,a simple piece of code that divides two numbers.

Everything seems correct on the compiler.

Variable names are clear and commas and semicolons are in the right places.

As a result,the compiler doesn't flag any syntax errors,meaning there's nothing wrong with the code structure or formatting.

However,if this code runs and the user passes zero as the denominator argument,a runtime error will occur.

You will find a runtime error only when the program executes.

The error would occur in this case because having a zero in the denominator is a mathematical impossibility.

To fix this,many programmers use a debugger.

A debugger is a tool that helps developers find and fix errors or bugs in their code.

It allows you to run your code step by step,inspect variables,and understand the flow of execution to identify where things go wrong.

One of the most popular debuggers front-end developers use is integrated into Visual Studio Code,VS Code,and you can access it through the debugger button in the top right corner.

In the VS Code debugger,you can set breakpoints to stop the program's execution,watch variables,and step through your code to understand exactly how it runs.

This makes VS Code a powerful tool for quickly identifying and fixing errors,which improves your code's quality and reliability.

Let's find out how this works in our example where we're dividing two numbers.

We built our divide Numbers method.

It gets two parameters and returns their quotient.

In this example,we'll execute this code and call the method with a zero in the denominator.

To do this,I store the result in a variable called result.

Here we are dividing the numerator 10 with the denominator 0,and then we print the result.

The code compiles,so there aren't any syntax errors.

But when I run the program,it shows the result is and then an infinite sign.

This is a runtime error.

When faced with an error like this,you can count on several debugger features to help you debug the code.

The first one is breakpoints.

Here is the code for the divide Numbers method.

In VS Code,you can set breakpoints by clicking in the gutter next to the line number where you want to pause the execution.

We can add a breakpoint in this line.

The next time the program runs this line,it will stop before executing,so you can determine what may be happening before this breakpoint.

In general,you want to add breakpoints on the lines where your variables change so you can inspect their values later and review whether they are as expected.

After setting your breakpoints,the next step is inspecting your variables.

This is where you can determine exactly what's happening in your code at that precise moment when the breakpoint is hit.

Checking the values of variables during execution helps you verify that your program is manipulating data as expected.

The debugger includes a tool called the Inspector.

You can use it to hover over a variable to reveal what value it's holding.

In this case,the denominator has a zero.

Once you have checked the variables,you can manually move to the following line using the step through code feature in the debugger.

This lets you move through the code line by line using the toolbar controls to follow exactly how things are flowing and to catch where things might be going wrong.

The debugger toolbar shows a set of buttons that you can use.

The first button is continue,which resumes program execution until the next breakpoint is hit or the program ends.

The next button is step over,which executes the current line of code and moves to the next line,but does not enter into functions or method called by that line.

Then the step into button.

This executes the current line of code and if the line contains a function or method,enters into that function to allow line-by-line debugging inside it.

Next,the step out button,which completes the execution of the current function and returns to the calling function,then pauses.

Then the restart button,which stops the current debugging session and starts it again from the beginning.

Finally,the stop button ends the current debugging session entirely,halting all code execution.

Stepping through code works once the debugger has stopped the execution as mandated by a breakpoint.

In this example,I've added a variable called result because I want to inspect the result of the operation.

To do so,I've added a breakpoint in this line.

At this point,you can inspect the variables and check what is happening inside your code.

If you want,you can step through it to find out exactly where the error occurs.

In this case,we notice the variable result has a zero,which would cause the error we're investigating.

Stepping through the code allows you to pinpoint exactly where issues occur.

For example,when I step over this line and inspect the variable now,I get the odd result of having a value of infinity,which was the unexpected behavior to begin with.

With the right focus,you can locate the errors in your code.

Using the debugger's inspector will help you understand the values of variables to get more information on them.

In this video,we explored the importance of debugging techniques to efficiently identify and fix errors in your code.

Solving abilities and become a more competent,efficient programmer.

02-04.01_C#基础语法

在学习一门新的编程语言时，从一开始就掌握正确的结构和语法，是决定后续学习是顺利推进还是陷入无尽调试的关键。

在开始学习C#时，理解程序的每个部分如何组合在一起，对于编写干净、实用的代码至关重要。

现在掌握这些基础知识，将为你未来构建的一切奠定基础。

在本视频中，我们将解释C#程序的基本语法和结构。

我们会涵盖关键概念，例如类、变量、控制结构和方法。

我们还将通过一些简单的代码示例来说明这些概念。

首先，让我们来看看一个基础C#程序的结构。

C#程序以类定义开始，并包含存放实际逻辑的方法。

这个C#代码块定义了程序类，其中包含了所有可执行的代码。

静态void Main方法是程序的入口点。

花括号定义了类和方法所包含的代码块。

控制台点写入行命令，会将文本“hello world”输出到控制台。

这就是程序的主体，实际工作在这里完成。

接下来，我们将学习变量，它用于存储数据，以便在程序中使用和操作。

变量需要使用类型关键字进行声明，这能告诉程序该变量存放的是哪种类型的数据。

有一个例外情况，我们稍后会讲到。

这段示例代码声明了四个不同的变量，并给它们初始化了一个值。

首先是“整数年龄等于25”，声明了一个名为age的整数变量，并为其赋值为25。

整数数据类型只能存放整数数据。

第二个变量name，我们使用字符串数据类型来声明。

我们将文本“John”也赋给了name变量。

字符串变量只能存放文本数据。

最后两个变量使用了特殊的关键字“var”。

这些变量没有预先声明类型。

而是由C#在程序运行时，来确定变量存放的是什么类型的值。

在这个例子中，有一个名为count的变量，它被初始化为整数10；另一个名为message的变量，被初始化为字符串值“hello”。

两者都是用关键字“var”声明的。

现在，我们来学习如何在C#中创建控制结构。

控制结构管理程序的流程。

最常见的控制结构是if语句和循环。

这些结构帮助程序做出决策和重复执行操作。

我们先从if-else语句开始，它用于判断真/假条件。

在这段示例代码中，语句“如果年龄大于或等于18”检查age是否大于或等于18。

注意，我们同时使用了大于号和等于号来检查年龄是否达到或超过18岁，而不仅仅是大于18岁。

如果该条件为真，程序会打印“您是成年人”。

如果该条件为假，程序会转到else部分的代码，并打印“您是未成年人”。

这种结构允许程序根据条件执行不同的操作。

下一种控制结构是循环，你可以用它来重复运行同一段代码块。

让我们看看循环在C#中是如何工作的。

在C#中，for循环是用关键字“for”来定义的。

你想要重复运行的代码放在花括号内。

然后，我们在声明关键字后面的圆括号内定义循环的条件。

在这个代码示例中，我们首先将迭代器i初始化为0。

这设置了循环的起始值。

然后我们设置循环何时退出的条件。

这里，我们告诉循环仅在i小于5时运行。

最后，我们使用代码“i加加”，在每次循环时给变量i加上1。

在循环内部，我们编写代码“控制台点写入行i”，这会将i的值打印到控制台。

这个示例会将数字0到4打印到控制台。

方法是我们将要学习的最后一个概念。

方法是在像C#这样的面向对象语言中，存在于工作类中的函数。

它们是执行特定任务的代码块。

它们可以接受参数并返回值。

这有助于组织代码并使其可复用。

我们来探讨一下它们是如何工作以及如何构建的。

在这个代码示例中，我们创建了一个名为“计算器”的类。

我们可以向这个类添加代码，使其能够执行计算器的所有操作。

通过将那些功能添加到一个类中，我们把执行相似工作的代码集中到一个地方，并让它们能够协同工作。

该类有两个成员变量，分别叫做number1和number2，它们的数据类型是int。

这些变量被声明为私有，因为它们只会被类中的方法使用。

在这个例子中，我们将向类添加一个名为Add的方法，它被声明为int类型。

这样声明方法意味着，该方法返回给调用它的代码的值，必须是int数据类型。

因为我们是在做加法，所以我们实现了两个整数类型的参数。

这些是将要传入方法的数据。

现在，我们在Add方法中，将成员变量赋值为输入参数的值。

由于成员变量被声明为类的一部分，因此方法可以使用它们来完成工作。

这就是方法和函数之间的区别之一。

方法可以使用类的成员，而函数则不能。

最后，方法的主体将参数number1和number2的值相加，并返回结果。

这个方法操作起来类似于函数。

通过处理数据并返回结果。

但你会注意到，它在类内部工作，并且可以与其他类成员交互和操作。

现在，让我们通过构建我们的计算器程序，将所有概念融会贯通。

我们将使用上一个例子中类似的类和方法，声明一个包含Add方法的Calculator类。

接下来，我们添加第二个静态方法，叫做Main。

我们将Main声明为void返回类型，因为这个方法不接受输入参数，也不返回值。

现在，我们添加两个成员变量num1和num2，声明为整数类型。

我们将num1初始化为值5，将num2初始化为值10。

接着，我们声明一个名为result的变量，类型为int。

我们将它的值设置为调用Add方法返回的结果。

我们将num1和num2的值作为参数传递给Add方法。

最后，我们使用“控制台点写入行”将result变量的值打印到控制台。

在这个例子中，结果将是15，所以控制台会显示“总和是15”。

至此，我们已经讲解了C#程序的基本语法和结构。

First,let's examine the structure of a basic C#program.

C#programs start with a class definition and contain methods where the actual logic resides.

This C#code block defines the program class,which contains all the executable code.

The static void main method serves as the entry point for the program.

The braces define the blocks of code for the class and method.

The console.write Line command outputs the text hello world to the console.

This is the program's body where the work is done.

There is an exception which we'll examine shortly.

Integer data types can only hold data that are whole numbers.

The second variable name we declare with the string data type.

We also assign the text John to the name variable.

String variables can only hold text data.

Our final two variables use a special type keyword,var.

These variables don't declare a type up front.

Both are declared with the type keyword var.

Control structures manage the flow of the program.

These structures help the program make decisions and repeat actions.

In this sample code,the statement if age is greater than or equal to 18 checks whether age is greater than or equal to 18.

If that statement is false,the program moves to the code in the else section of the code and prints,you're a minor.

This structure allows the program to take different actions based on the condition.

The next type of control structure is the loop,which you can use to run the same block of code repeatedly.

In C#,a for loop is defined with the for keyword.

The code you want to run repeatedly is put between the curly braces.

Then we define the conditions for the loop in the parentheses after the declaration keyword.

In this code sample,we first initialize the iterator i to 0.

This sets the starting value of the loop.

Then we set the condition for when the loop will exit.

Here,we tell the loop to run only as long as i is less than 5.

Finally,we use the code i to add 1 to the variable i each time through the loop.

Inside the loop,we write the code console.write line i,which prints the value of i to the console.

This sample will print the numbers 0 to 4 to the console.

Methods are functions that exist in working classes in an object-oriented language like C#.

They are blocks of code that perform specific tasks.

They can take parameters and return values.

This helps organize the code and make it reusable.

Let's investigate how they work and how they're constructed.

In this code sample,we create a class called calculator.

We can add code to this class that can perform all the operations of a calculator.

By adding that functionality to a class,we group code that does similar work into one place and give it the ability to work together.

The class has two member variables called number one and number two that have the int data type.

These are declared as private since they'll only be used by the methods in the class.

For this example,we'll add one method to the class called add,which is declared as an int.

Declaring the method this way means that the value the method returns to the code that called it has to be an int data type.

Because we're adding numbers,we implemented two integer parameters.

These are the data that will pass into the method.

Now we'll assign the member variables to the value of the input parameters in the add method.

Because the member variables were declared as a part of the class,the method can use them to do its work.

This is one of the differences between a method and a function.

A method can use members of the class and a function cannot.

Finally,the body of the method sums the values of the parameters number 1 and number 2 and returns the result.

This method operates similarly to a function.

By processing data and returning a result.

But you'll notice that it works inside a class and can interact with and operate on other class members.

Now let's bring all these concepts together by building on our calculator program.

Next,we'll add a second static method called main.

Next,we declare a variable called result with the type int.

Finally,we use console.write Line to print the value of the result variable to the console.

In this example,the result will be 15,so the console will show the sum is 15.

Then we use the method console.readline,which detects characters in the terminal until the user hits the enter key.

The program then greets you by saying hello and then your name.

As usual,we write a main method to call the greet user method.

When you run this program,it will ask you for your name and greets you.

Let's say I type my name here and hit enter,and then the program will greet me.

Hello,computer,nice to meet you.

These exercises have provided a hands-on approach to understanding C#syntax and structure.

02-04.04_用C#编写简单程序

在第一步中，我们将创建一个简单的计算器程序，它可以执行基本的算术运算，比如加法和减法。

我们将从定义一个类并编写一个方法来对两个数相加开始。

在C#中，类就像是你程序代码的一个容器。

它包含方法，方法是一组指令，告诉程序要做什么。

可以把方法想象成程序一步步遵循的食谱。

这里我们要添加一个叫做Add的方法，它会把两个数加在一起。

单词static意味着这个方法属于类本身，而public意味着它可以从程序的其他部分被访问。

变量就像一个容器，它保存着程序可以使用的数据。

这里我们有两个变量，叫做number One和number Two，它们将存储我们要相加的数字。

该方法将使用return关键字返回结果，这意味着该方法将答案发送回调用它的代码部分。

现在我们已经创建了Add方法，是时候看看它是如何工作的了。

要执行一个C#程序，我们需要编写一个Main方法，它是程序的入口。

这是所有部分汇集的地方。

在C#中，每个程序都是从运行Main方法开始的，它就像程序的起点。

当你运行一个C#程序时，Main方法内部的代码是最先执行的。

在这种情况下，在Main方法内部，我们将为number One和number Two赋值，调用Add方法来计算总和，并使用控制台点写入一行（console.Write Line）将结果打印在屏幕上。

调用一个方法意味着运行该方法内部的代码，并使用其结果。

接下来，我们将编写一个程序，使用循环来显示从1到10的数字。

循环是允许你多次重复执行操作的控制结构。

当我们知道想要重复执行一个代码块的次数时，会使用for循环。

在这种情况下，我们想要打印从1到10的数字。

循环有三个部分。

初始化，整数i等于1。

这设置了循环的起始点。

我们从i等于1开始。

条件，i小于或等于10。

这告诉循环何时停止。

只要i小于或等于10，循环就会运行。

当i变成11时，循环将停止。

增量，i加加。

这在每次循环迭代后将i的值增加1，这样下一个数字就会被打印出来。

现在让我们编写一个与用户交互的程序。

这个程序将提示用户输入他们的名字，并向他们问好。

处理用户输入对于创建交互式程序非常重要。

在这一步中，我们将通过询问用户的名字来与他们互动。

要捕获用户的输入，我们使用控制台点读取一行（console.Read Line）。

这会等待用户输入一些内容，然后将该输入存储为一个字符串，字符串是一个字符序列，比如一个单词或句子。

我们将用户的输入存储在一个名为name的变量中。

一旦我们将用户的名字存储在name中，我们就可以使用控制台点写入一行（console.Write Line）来显示个性化的问候语。

注意，我们首先用控制台点写入一行（console.Write Line）提示用户输入他们的名字。

用户输入他们的名字后，使用控制台点读取一行（console.Read Line）将其存储在name变量中。

然后我们使用那个变量，通过名字来问候用户，展示了输入是如何被动态使用的。

控制台点读取一行（console.Read Line）用于捕获输入，而控制台点写入一行（console.Write Line）用于向用户显示消息。

英文原文

In this first step,we will create a simple calculator program that performs basic arithmetic operations such as addition and subtraction.

We will start by defining a class and writing a method to add two numbers.

In C#,a class is like a container for your program's code.

It holds methods,which are sets of instructions that tell the program what to do.

Think of a method like a recipe that the program follows step by step.

Here we're going to add a method called add,which will add two numbers together.

The word static means the method belongs to the class itself,and public means it can be accessed from other parts of the program.

A variable is like a container that holds data that your program can use.

Here we have two variables called number one and number two that will store the numbers we want to add.

The method will return the result using the return keyword,which means the method sends the answer back to the part of code that called it.

Now that we've created the add method,it's time to see how it works.

To execute a C#program,we need to write a main method,which is the entry port of the program.

This is where everything comes together.

In C#,every program starts by running the main method,which is like the starting point of the program.

When you run a C#program,the code inside main is the first to execute.

In this case,inside main,we're going to assign values to number 1 and number 2,call the add method to calculate the sum,and use console.write Line to print the results on the screen.

Calling a method means running the code inside the method and using its result.

Next,we will write a program that uses a loop to display numbers from 1 to 10.

Loops are control structures that allow you to repeat actions multiple times.

A for loop is used when we know how many times we want to repeat a block of code.

In this case,we want to print numbers from 1 to 10.

The loop has three parts.

Initialization,int i equals 1.

This sets the starting point for the loop.

We start with i equals 1.

Condition,i is less than or equal to 10.

This tells the loop when to stop.

As long as i is less than or equal to 10,the loop will run.

When i becomes 11,the loop will stop.

Increment,i plus plus.

This increases the value of i by 1 after each loop iteration,so the next number is printed.

Now let's write a program that interacts with the user.

This program will prompt the user for their name and greet them.

Handling user input is important for creating interactive programs.

In this step,we'll interact with the user by asking for their name.

To capture the user's input,we use console.readline.

This waits for the user to type something and then stores that input as a string,which is a sequence of characters like a word or sentence.

We store the user's input in a variable called name.

Once we have the user's name stored in name,we can use console.write Line to display a personalized greeting.

Notice that we first prompt the user to enter their name with console.write Line.

After the user types their name,it is stored in the name variable using console.readline.

We then use that variable to greet the user by name,showing how input can be used dynamically.

Console.readline is useful for capturing input while console.writeline is used to display messages to the user.

02-05.01_创建一个小项目并调试程序

我们将创建一个简单的控制台应用程序，让用户可以管理一个简单的待办事项列表。

这个程序将允许用户添加任务、查看任务列表，以及将任务标记为已完成。

这涉及到收集用户输入、将数据存储在一个数组中，以及使用循环来与列表进行交互。

在构建程序时，将其分解为更小的任务会很有帮助，这样更容易编写和理解。

在这个程序中，我们将任务添加到一个列表，查看列表以了解有哪些任务，以及在任务完成时将其标记为已完成。

为了实现这一点，我们将使用方法、循环和数组。

程序的每个部分都将执行其自身的任务，使得整体处理起来更容易。

数组很有用，因为它们允许我们存储多个任务，而循环则允许我们反复检查用户接下来想要做什么。

最后，我们将使用调试技术来捕捉并修复我们可能犯的任何错误。

首先，我们需要一个地方来存储这些任务。

我们将使用一个数组，这是一种特殊类型的变量，可以存储多条数据。

当我们想要存储多个相同类型的事物时，数组非常有用。

在C#中，数组就像一个容器，它保存着多个相同类型的值。

在我们的例子中，数组将保存字符串，也就是代表任务的文本片段。

数组有固定的大小，在这种情况下是10个任务，这意味着我们最多只能在数组中存储10个任务。

每个任务将存储在一个独立的位置，称为索引。

我们使用从零开始计数的索引来访问数组中的每个任务。

我们还需要一个名为任务计数（task count）的计数器变量来跟踪列表中有多少任务。

这个变量从零开始，因为一开始列表是空的。

每次添加一个任务时，任务计数（task count）就增加一，确保下一个任务存储在数组中下一个可用的位置。

接下来，我们将创建一个方法，让用户可以向列表中添加任务。

当用户输入一个新任务时，我们将使用任务计数（task count）变量将其存储在数组的正确位置上。

我们将使用一个名为添加任务（add task）的方法来提示用户输入一个任务。

该方法将使用控制台点读取一行（console.readline）来捕获用户输入的内容，并将此任务存储在数组中。

该任务将保存在数组中下一个可用的位置，这个位置由任务计数（task Count）决定。

记住，数组从索引0开始，所以第一个任务将存储在任务数组索引0处（tasks 0），第二个在索引1处（tasks 1），依此类推。

每添加一个任务后，我们增加任务计数（task count）。

将任务计数（task count）增加一，以确保下一个任务进入下一个可用位置。

这确保了每个新任务都被添加到正确的位置，并且我们不会覆盖任何现有的任务。

任务计数（task count）变量帮助我们跟踪已添加了多少个任务，以及下一个任务应该放在哪里。

现在我们将创建一个方法来显示用户迄今为止添加的所有任务。

这个方法将循环遍历任务数组（tasks array）并打印出每个任务。

要查看任务，我们将使用一个循环来遍历任务数组（tasks array）中的每个元素。

循环将从索引0（数组的开头）开始，一旦达到任务计数（task count）就停止。

这确保了我们只打印已添加的任务，并避免打印数组中的空位置。

每个任务将带有一个从1开始的编号进行打印，以便于阅读，尽管数组索引是从0开始的。

使用索引i加1是为了让用户界面更友好。

现在我们将创建一个方法，让用户可以将任务标记为已完成。

这将允许用户更新数组中的某个任务，并表明该任务已经完成。

用户将通过输入任务的编号来选择要标记为已完成的任务。

C#中的数组从0开始计数，但用户将输入从1开始的数字，因此我们需要从他们的输入中减去1，以获得数组中的正确索引。

例如，如果用户选择了任务1，我们需要更新任务数组索引0处的元素（tasks 0）。

一旦我们在数组中找到正确的任务，我们将通过添加“已完成”文本来修改它，以表明它已完成。

这将更新该任务，并向用户清楚地表明它已被标记为完成。

最后，我们将编写将一切整合起来的主方法（main method）。

这个方法将让用户决定是添加任务、查看任务，还是将任务标记为已完成。

我们将使用一个while循环来保持程序运行，直到用户决定退出。

在循环内部，程序将显示一个选项菜单。

用户可以选择一个选项来添加任务、查看任务或将任务标记为完成。

我们将使用一个开关语句（switch statement）来处理用户的输入，根据他们的选择调用相应的方法。

循环将持续进行，直到用户选择退出程序的选项。

在这一步中，我们将介绍一个程序中可能出现的常见错误，并逐步讲解查找和修复它的过程。

调试是一项重要的技能，学习如何识别和解决代码中的问题将使你成为一名更好的程序员。

假设用户试图将一个任务标记为已完成，但他们输入的数字超出了任务列表的范围。

例如，他们输入了12，但只有三个任务。

这将导致索引超出范围错误，因为程序试图访问数组中不存在的那部分。

让我们探讨一下如何调试并解决这个问题。

当你的程序试图访问数组中一个不存在的位置时，就会发生索引超出范围错误。

例如，如果你的数组有三个任务，而用户试图将任务4标记为完成，程序将会崩溃，因为该位置没有任务。

为了防止这种情况，我们可以使用错误检查来确保用户的输入在正确的范围内。

要修复这个错误，我们将在访问数组之前打印任务编号的值，以检查其是否正确。

然后，我们将更新代码，如果用户输入的数字超出了任务范围，就向他们显示一条错误消息。

一旦你添加了调试行并处理了该错误，当用户输入无效的任务编号时，你的程序就不应该再崩溃了。

相反，它应该显示一条错误消息，比如，“无效的任务编号，请重试。”

英文原文

We will create a small console application that allows users to manage a simple to-do list.

The program will let users add tasks,view the list of tasks,and mark tasks as completed.

This will involve collecting user input,storing the data in an array,and using loops to interact with the list.

When building a program,it's helpful to break it down into smaller tasks,which makes it easier to write and understand.

In this program,we will add tasks to a list,view tasks to see what's on the list,mark tasks as completed when they're finished.

To do this,we will use methods,loops,and arrays.

Each part of the program will have its own task to perform,making it easier to work with.

Arrays are useful because they allow us to store multiple tasks,and loops allow us to repeatedly check what the user wants to do next.

Finally,we'll use debugging techniques to catch and fix any mistakes we might make.

First,we need a place to store the tasks.

We will use an array,which is a special type of variable that can store multiple pieces of data.

Arrays are useful when we want to store several things of the same type.

In C#,an array is like a container that holds several values of the same type.

In our case,the array will hold strings,which are pieces of text representing the tasks.

Arrays have a fixed size,in this case,10 tasks,which means we can only store up to the 10 tasks in the array.

Each task will be stored in a separate spot called an index.

We access each task in the array using an index which starts counting from zero.

We'll also need a counter variable called task Count to keep track of how many tasks are in the list.

This variable starts at zero because the list is empty at first.

Each time a task is added,task count will be increased by one,making sure the next task is stored in the next available spot in the array.

Next,we will create a method to let the user add tasks to the list.

When the user types in a new task,we will store it in the array at the correct position using the task count variable.

We'll use a method called add task to ask the user to enter a task.

The method will store this task in the array using console.readline,which captures what the user types.

The task will be saved in the next available spot in the array,which is determined by task Count.

Remember,arrays start at index 0,so the first task will be stored in tasks 0,the second in tasks 1,and so on.

After each task is added,we increase the task.

Increased task count by one to make sure the next task goes into the next available spot.

This ensures that every new task is added to the right spot and we don't overwrite any existing tasks.

The task count variable helps us keep track of how many tasks have been added and where the next one should go.

Now we will create a method to display all the tasks the user has added so far.

This method will loop through the tasks array and print each task.

To view the tasks,we'll use a loop to go through each element in the tasks array.

The loop will start at index 0,the beginning of the array,and will stop once it reaches task count.

This ensures that we only print the tasks that have been added and avoid printing empty spots in the array.

Each task will be printed with a number starting from 1,for easier reading,even though array indices start from 0.

The number i plus 1 is used to make this more user-friendly.

Now we'll create a method to let the user mark a task as completed.

This will allow the user to update a task in the array and show it has been finished.

The user will choose a task to mark as completed by entering the task's number.

Arrays in C#start counting from 0,but the user will enter a number starting from 1,so we need to subtract 1 from their input to get the correct index in the array.

For example,if the user selects task 1,we need to update tasks 0.

Once we find the correct task in the array,we will modify it by adding the text completed to show it is finished.

This will update the task and make it clear to the user that it has been marked as complete.

Finally,we will write the main method that brings everything together.

This method will let the user decide whether to add a task,view tasks,or mark a task as completed.

We'll use a while loop to keep the program running until the user decides to quit.

Inside the loop,the program will show a menu of options.

The user can select an option to add a task,view task,or mark a task as complete.

We'll use a switch statement to handle the user's input,calling the right method based on their choice.

The loop will continue until the user selects the option to exit the program.

In this step,we will introduce a common error that can happen in your program and walk through the process of finding and fixing it.

Debugging is an important skill,and learning how to identify and solve problems in your code will make you a better programmer.

Let's say the user tries to mark a task as completed,but they enter a number that is outside the range of the task list.

For example,they enter 12,but there are only three tasks.

This will cause an index out of range error because the program tries to access a part of the array that doesn't exist.

Let's explore how we can debug and fix this problem.

An index out-of-range error occurs when your program tries to access a position in an array that doesn't exist.

For example,if your array has three tasks and the user tries to mark task 4 as complete,the program will crash because there is no task at that position.

To prevent this,we can use error checking to make sure the user's input is within the correct range.

To fix this error,we'll print the value of task number before accessing the array to check if it's correct.

Then we will update the code to give the user an error message if they enter a number outside the range of the tasks.

Once you've added the debugging line and handled the error,your program no longer should crash when the users enter an invalid task number.

Instead,it should display an error message like,invalid task number,please try again.

03-01.02_对象与类的基本概念

生物学中的一门学科是分类学。

科学家们使用特定的属性来对动物进行分组，从而创建出像斑马、鹈鹕和人类这样的类别。

在这些类别中，存在着一个个具体的斑马、鹈鹕和人类个体，它们共享着该类其他成员所具有的属性，比如条纹、鸟喙和能对生的拇指。

分类学帮助科学家们管理他们的研究，理解动物之间的异同，甚至了解它们的行为会有怎样的预期。

计算机科学家们已经将许多这些概念应用到了一个名为面向对象编程的编程模型中，简称OOP。

在本视频中，我们将解释面向对象编程中对象和类的基本概念。

对象和类是面向对象编程中基础的代码构建块。

基于代码的类和对象之间的关系，就如同一种动物与其所属类别之间的关系一样。

你可以把对象看作是一个单独的事物，或者说是类的一个实例，就像一匹特定的斑马。

而类则是一种定义类型。

它描述了一只普通斑马所拥有的全部属性和能力。

让我们更详细地探讨这些概念，先从类的定义开始。

类是为一组事物定义共享特征和行为的蓝图或模板。

代码中的类类似于生物学或植物学中的类别，它们定义了各个代码对象的属性以及它们能做什么。

面向对象编程中的类本身做不了太多工作。

程序员不能直接用类来处理数据、与用户交互或发送API调用。

但类提供了如何完成所有这些事情的蓝图。

具体来说，一个类有三个主要特征来定义它们。

开发者在类中定义的第一个特征是属性。

这些是对象存储或设置的单个数据项。

例如，一个身份验证类可能会定义像用户名和密码这样的属性。

类还定义方法，这些方法也被称为行为或函数。

方法是类可以执行的行为。

例如，一个登录类可能会定义像验证这样的方法，它检查用户的凭证；以及登录方法，它授予用户访问系统的权限。

最后，通过一个称为继承的特性，类可以被扩展以创建子类，这些子类从父类继承属性和行为，就像生物学中的后代一样。

这个特性使得开发者能够重用代码，并更好地组织代码。

例如，开发者可以继承登录类，重用它的属性和方法，但添加一个名为显示名称的新属性，他们可以在用户登录后在用户界面上显示这个属性。

因为类仅仅是定义，所以为了让它们工作，开发者必须创建它们的可工作版本。

这被称为实例化。

这个过程会创建出类的独立对象或者说实例。

对象是一个独特的事物，它拥有定义其特征和动作的独特属性和行为。

代码对象，就像其他类型的对象一样，由它们的属性以及它们在程序中所能做的事情来定义。

代码对象拥有其所实例化的那个类的所有属性和方法。

对象的属性是与该对象相关联的数据元素，由类中的变量来定义。

它的方法是该对象可以执行的操作或函数，由类中的方法来定义。

对象还有一些其他特征，使它们与父类区分开来。

每个对象在程序内部都有一个唯一的标识，通常由其内存地址或唯一标识符来表示。

因为对象是类的实例，开发者可以根据需要创建任意多个，并且每个对象在应用程序中都有唯一的标识。

对象的状态由其属性的当前值决定。

例如，一个登录对象的状态可能包括它当前持有的用户名和显示名称的值。

一个对象只能执行其父类中定义的操作，除非开发者使用继承来扩展该类。

那么，使用继承类创建的对象将拥有所有新的属性和方法，以及来自父类的原始属性和方法。

现在，让我们用一个可能在图书馆系统中使用的图书类的例子，把这些概念串联起来。

图书类可能有标题、作者和ISBN这样的属性。

它也可能有借出和归还这样的方法。

通过定义这些属性和方法，我们可以创建具体的图书对象，每个对象代表图书馆里的一本特定图书，拥有自己的标题、作者和ISBN。

当一本书被借出时，借出方法可以更新该书的状态，这演示了在面向对象编程中，属性和方法如何协同工作来管理对象的状态和行为。

面向对象编程是一种高效且强大的编程模型。

通过定义类和创建对象，开发者可以编写出更易于维护和重用的代码。

英文原文

One of the disciplines in biology is classification.

Scientists use specific properties to group animals to create classes like zebras,pelicans,and humans.

Within these classes are individual zebras,pelicans,and humans that share properties of other members of the class,like stripes,beaks,and opposable thumbs.

Classification helps scientists manage their research,understand how animals are similar and different,and even know what to expect from their behavior.

Computer scientists have applied many of these concepts in a programming model called Object-Oriented Programming,or OOP.

In this video,we will explain the basic concepts of objects and classes in OOP.

Objects and classes are the fundamental coding building blocks of object-oriented programming.

Code-based classes and objects are related in the same way that an animal is related to the class in which it belongs.

Think of an object as an individual thing or an instance of a class,like a particular zebra.

The class is a type of definition.

It describes all the properties and abilities that the average zebra has.

Let's explore these concepts in more detail,starting with the definition of a class.

A class is a blueprint or template that defines a set of shared characteristics and behaviors for a group of things.

Code classes are similar to classes in biology or botany in that they define the properties of the individual code objects and what they can do.

Classes in object-oriented programming by themselves can't do much work.

A programmer can't process data,interact with the user,or send API calls.

But a class provides a blueprint for how to do all these things.

Specifically,a class has three main features that define them.

The first feature developers define in classes are properties.

These are the individual items of data that an object stores or sets.

For example,an authentication class may define properties like username and password.

Classes also define methods which are also referred to as behaviors or functions.

Methods are the behaviors the class can perform.

For example,the login class may define methods like validate,which checks the user's credentials,and login,which gives the user access to the system.

Finally,through a feature called inheritance,classes can be extended to create subclasses that inherit properties and behaviors from the parent class,just like offspring in biology.

This feature enables the developer to reuse and better organized code.

For example,a developer may inherit the login class,reusing its properties and methods,but adding a new attribute called display name that they can show on a user interface after the user has logged in.

Because classes are definitions only,in order for them to do work,developers have to create a working version of them.

This is called instantiation.

and the process creates individual objects or instances of the class.

An object is a distinct thing that possesses unique properties and behaviors that define its characteristics and actions.

Code objects,like other types of objects,are defined by their properties and the things they can do within a program.

Code objects have all the properties and methods of the class of which they are an instance.

The object's properties are the data elements associated with the object defined by variables within the class.

Its methods are the operations or functions the object can perform defined by the methods in the class.

Objects also have a couple of other features that differentiate them from the parent classes.

Each object has a unique identity within the program,often represented by its memory address or a unique identifier.

Because objects are instances of classes,the developer can create as many as they need,and each one is uniquely identified in the application.

The object's state is determined by the current values of its properties.

For example,a login object's state might include the values it currently holds for username and display name.

An object can only do what is defined in its parent class unless a developer uses inheritance to extend the class.

Then an object created with an inherited class will have all the new properties and methods,as well as the original one from the parent.

Now let's bring these ideas together using an example of a book class that might be used in a library system.

The book class might have properties like title,author,and ISBN.

It might also have methods like borrow and return.

By defining these properties and methods,we can create specific book objects,each representing a particular book in the library with its own title,author,and ISBN.

When a book is borrowed,the borrow method can update the status of the book,demonstrating how properties and methods work together to manage the state and behavior of objects in OOP.

Object-oriented programming is an efficient and powerful programming model.

By defining classes and creating objects,developers can write code that's more easily maintainable and reusable.

03-01.03_面向对象原则的理论理解

就像你不需要了解邮局的内部运作原理也能使用它的服务一样，开发者也不需要知道一个类的内部细节就能与之交互。

开发者运用封装和抽象的原则来简化他们代码的使用方式。

在本视频中，你将学习如何解释面向对象编程中封装和抽象的原则，以及它们如何保护对象的内部状态并简化其使用。

我们先从封装开始。

封装是将方法和数据捆绑到一个类中，并限制对类某些组件的访问的原则。

这是通过管理可以访问和修改类数据的接口来实现的。

当开发者应用这一原则时，他们就将对象的内部状态对外部代码隐藏起来，只暴露必要的内容。

让我们更仔细地研究开发者如何使用这个原则来创建类。

封装通过管理对其属性和方法的访问来保护对象的内部状态。

开发者使用像private、protected和public这样的访问修饰符来管理其他代码可以访问哪些数据和方法，以及数据如何被修改。

例如，假设一家银行开发了一个应用程序，代码中有一个银行账户类，其中包含用于管理与客户账户相关的数据和方法。

余额属性存储客户的账户余额数据。

开发者可能会提供一个公共的获取余额方法，让其他代码可以读取该属性，但不提供任何写入该属性的方法。

封装允许开发者使用公共方法将对象的某些部分暴露给应用程序的任何代码。

他们也可以使用受保护的方法，将其他部分只暴露给应用程序中继承自该类的部分。

这些原则让开发者可以精细地控制类的哪些部分被暴露，以及暴露的范围有多大。

假设银行账户类有一个利率属性，存储银行当前对该账户适用的利率。

开发者不想暴露利率属性，所以他们不创建任何访问它的方法。

但是，他们希望人们能够进行存款和取款，因此他们提供了实现这些活动的公共方法。

封装是一个专注于管理类的数据和方法的原則。

它帮助开发者保护他们想要保持安全的类部分，并暴露应用程序其余部分可以使用的部分。

面向对象编程的另一个原则，抽象，定义了类应该实现哪些方法，而不提供具体的实现细节。

我们接下来探讨这个。

抽象是定义对象做什么，但不指定它怎么做的原则。

开发者通过创建抽象类和接口来实现这一点。

当开发者应用这一原则时，他们定义了对象的基本特征和行为，但让开发者来决定这些对象内部的方法如何实现。

让我们更仔细地研究开发者如何使用抽象来创建对象。

开发者可以通过两种主要方式应用抽象原则。

当他们想要共享一个包含具体方法的通用代码库时，可以使用抽象类，具体方法是那些包含了实现代码的方法。

但他们也可以包含抽象方法，这些方法只定义接口，但不提供任何实现细节。

开发者也可以创建不包含具体方法的接口。

你可以把接口想象成一个契约，它定义了一组继承类必须实现的方法。

接口通常只包含继承类必须实现哪些方法，但不包含实现代码。

假设在银行应用中，开发团队创建了一个用于处理存款和取款的抽象类。

这个类定义了一个金额属性，并声明了存款和取款的抽象方法。

储蓄账户功能继承自这个抽象类，并为如何处理存款和取款提供了具体的逻辑。

支票账户代码也是如此。

与应用中这些账户交互的任何其他部分都知道，储蓄账户和支票账户都会有存款和取款方法，而无需关心它们的实现细节。

现在让我们看看封装和抽象的原则是如何协同工作的。

假设一个图书馆创建了管理其库存的软件。

该软件的程序员创建了一个图书馆类，封装了关于书籍和借阅者的数据。

它使用私有属性隐藏内部数据结构，并提供像添加书籍和借阅书籍这样的公共方法。

这在允许受控访问的同时，保护了图书馆数据的完整性。

此外，它通过定义一个名为目录的接口来使用抽象，该接口暴露了像搜索书籍和列出可用书籍这样的方法。

任何继承自目录的类都实现其方法，使系统更易于使用和维护。

在本视频中，你学习了如何解释面向对象编程中封装和抽象的原则，以及它们如何保护对象的内部状态并简化其使用。

封装通过将数据和方法捆绑在类内部来保护对象的内部状态。

抽象帮助开发者创建一致、可预测但又灵活的类。

通过应用这些原则，开发者可以编写出安全且可维护的代码。

英文原文

Just as you don't need to understand the inner workings of a post office to use its services,developers don't need to know the internal details of a class to interact with it.

Developers use the principles of encapsulation and abstraction to simplify how their code is used.

In this video,you'll learn how to explain the principles of encapsulation and abstraction in object-oriented programming,and how these protect the internal state of an object and simplify its use.

Let's start with encapsulation.

Encapsulation is the principle of bundling methods and data into a class and restricting access to some of the class's components.

This is done by managing the interfaces through which the class's data can be accessed and modified.

When developers apply this principle,they keep an object's internal state hidden from outside code,exposing only what is necessary.

Let's examine more closely how developers use this principle to create classes.

Encapsulation protects an object's internal state by managing access to its attributes and methods.

Developers use access modifiers like private,protected,and public to manage what data and methods other code has access to and how data can be modified.

For example,suppose a bank developed an application and the code has a bank account class that includes data and methods for managing data related to a customer's account.

The balance attribute stores the customer's account balance data.

The developer might provide a public get balance method that lets other code read the attribute,but no method for writing to the attribute.

Encapsulation allows developers to expose parts of the object to any of the application's code using public methods.

They also can expose other parts only to parts of the app that inherit from the class using protected methods.

These principles give the developer fine-tuned control on which parts of the class are exposed and how broadly.

Suppose the bank account class has an interest rate attribute that stores the interest rate the bank currently applies to the account.

The developer doesn't want the interest rate attribute exposed,so they create no method for accessing it.

However,they do want people to be able to make deposits and withdrawals,and so they provide public methods that enable these activities.

Encapsulation is a principle that focuses on managing the data and methods of the class.

It helps developers protect parts of the class they want to keep secure and expose the part that the rest of the application can use.

Another principle of object-oriented programming,abstraction,defines what methods a class should implement without providing specific implementation details.

Let's explore that next.

Abstraction is the principle of defining what an object does but not specifying how it does it.

Developers achieve this by creating abstract classes and interfaces.

When developers apply this principle,they define an object's essential features and behaviors,but enable developers to decide how methods within those objects are implemented.

Let's examine more closely how developers use abstraction to create objects.

Developers can apply the principle of abstraction in two primary ways.

They can use an abstract class when they want to share a common code base with concrete methods,which are methods that include the implementation code.

But they also can include abstract methods that just define the interface,but don't provide any implementation details.

Developers can also create interfaces that do not include concrete methods.

You can think of an interface like a contract that defines a set of methods that an inherited class must implement.

Interfaces typically only include what methods an inherited class must implement,but not the implementation code.

Suppose in the banking application,the development team creates an abstract class for handling deposits and withdrawals.

This class defines an amount attribute and declares the abstract methods for deposit and withdrawal.

The savings account functionality inherits from this abstract class and provides specific logic for how deposits and withdrawals should be handled.

The checking account code does the same.

Any other part of the app that interacts with these accounts knows that both savings and checking will have deposits and withdrawal methods without needing to be concerned with their implementation details.

Now let's look at how the principles of encapsulation and abstraction work together.

Suppose a library creates software to manage its inventory.

The software's programmers create a library class that encapsulates data about books and patrons.

It hides the internal data structures using private attributes and provides public methods like add book and borrow book.

This protects the integrity of the library's data while allowing controlled access.

Additionally,it uses abstraction by defining an interface catalog that exposes methods like search book and list available books.

Any class that inherits from catalog implements its methods,making the system easier to use and maintain.

In this video,you learned how to explain the principles of encapsulation and abstraction in object-oriented programming,and how these protect the internal state of an object and simplify its use.

Encapsulation protects the internal state of an object by bundling data and methods within a class.

Abstraction helps developers create consistent,predictable,yet flexible classes.

Applying these principles,developers can write secure and maintainable code.

03-02.01_继承

你是否曾经希望可以不从头开始，就能把旧的东西改进得更好？

面向对象编程通过继承的概念解决了这个问题，它使开发者能够重用代码，但只根据需要进行修改和扩展。

在本视频中，我们将描述面向对象编程中继承的概念。

我们先从继承的定义开始。

继承是面向对象编程中的一个基本概念，它使开发者能够基于现有的类创建新类。

类似于生物学中的遗传，代码继承使得类能够从其他类继承特性。

它允许一个称为派生类的新类，从现有的类中继承属性和方法。

这个现有的类被称为超类或基类。

当开发者创建一个派生类时，他们获得了基类的属性和方法，但也可以通过添加自己的属性和方法来扩展他们的类。

这使得开发者可以重用基类中的代码，而无需重新编写。

他们还可以向类中添加更多功能，甚至在需要时修改原始的属性和方法。

这些新的和修改后的功能被隔离在派生类中，不会影响基类。

派生类和基类之间的关系类似于孩子和父母之间的关系。

孩子可能拥有父母的许多相似特征，比如肤色、鼻型、身高，但孩子也会发展出独特的特征，比如发色和个性。

这种关系在代码中创建了一个层次结构，促进了重用。

它也使得开发者能够更有效地组织和管理代码。

继承在类之间创建了一种树状结构。

例如，鸟类可以充当鹰和鹦鹉这些派生类的基类。

鹰和鹦鹉都会从鸟类继承共同的属性和方法，比如飞翔，但也可以拥有自己独特的属性。

鹰这个派生类可能包含一个狩猎方法，而鹦鹉这个派生类可能包含一个说话方法。

这些额外的方法不会成为基类的一部分，而是被隔离在派生类中。

通过使用继承，开发者可以重用现有代码，并用额外的功能对其进行扩展。

这促进了代码重用并减少了冗余。

在本视频中，你学习了描述面向对象编程中继承的概念。

继承使得开发者能够从现有类继承属性和方法，并创建层次关系。

通过将这一概念应用到他们的应用程序中，开发者可以通过促进现有功能的重用和扩展来编写高效且组织良好的代码。

英文原文

Have you ever wished you could take something old and make it better without starting from scratch?

Object-oriented programming addresses this through the concept of inheritance,which enables developers to reuse code but modify it and extend it only as needed.

In this video,we will describe the concept of inheritance in object-oriented programming.

Let's begin with the definition of inheritance.

Inheritance is a fundamental concept in object-oriented programming that enables developers to create new classes based on existing ones.

Similar to inheritance in biology,code inheritance enables classes to inherit features from other classes.

It allows a new class known as a derived class to inherit attributes and methods from the existing class.

This is called a superclass or base class.

When a developer creates a derived class,they get the attributes and methods of the base class,but also can extend their class by adding their own attributes and methods.

This allows the developer to reuse the code from the base class without having to reproduce it.

They also can add more features to the class and even modify the original attributes and methods if they need to.

The new and modified features are isolated to the derived class and don't impact the base class.

The relationship between the derived class and the base class is similar to the relationship between a child and a parent.

The child may have many similar features of the parents,such as skin color,nose,shape,and height,but the child will develop unique characteristics such as hair color and personality.

This relationship creates a hierarchy in code that fosters reuse.

It also allows developers to organize and manage code more efficiently.

Inheritance creates a tree-like structure between classes.

For instance,a bird class can be a base class for eagle and parrot-derived classes.

Both eagle and parrot will inherit common attributes and methods from bird,like fly,but can also have their own unique properties.

The eagle-derived class may include a hunt method,and the parrot-derived class could include a speak method.

The additional methods do not become a part of the base class,but remain isolated to the derived classes.

By using inheritance,developers can reuse existing code and extend it with additional functionality.

This promotes code reuse and reduces redundancy.

In this video,you learned to describe the concept of inheritance in object-oriented programming.

Inheritance enables developers to inherit properties and methods from existing classes and create hierarchical relationships.

By applying this concept to their applications,developers can write efficient and organized code by promoting reuse and extension of existing functionalities.

03-02.02_多态

相比传统应用，现代消息应用在处理文件方面更为先进。

例如，当你分享一个视频文件时，你的消息应用可能会自动提供一个播放按钮，方便你的朋友或同事观看。

如果你上传照片，他们可能会看到一个翻页式的界面，可以滚动浏览所有图片。

应用为文件提供了一个统一的界面，但它会根据你上传的文件类型，自动调整以执行不同的操作。

面向对象编程为代码编写提供了类似的概念。

多态性使开发者能够创建一个方法，该方法根据其作用的对象不同，执行不同的任务。

在本视频中，你将学习如何解释面向对象编程中的多态性概念。

首先，我们来定义一下什么是多态性。

多态性使开发者能够使用同一个方法名称，根据其作用的对象类型来执行不同的任务。

这意味着一个方法可以根据其被要求执行的操作，表现出不同的行为。下面是一个在对象上应用多态性的例子。

开发者首先通过扩展一个基类来创建一个派生类。

通过继承，派生类拥有了基类的方法和属性。

此时，他们可以直接使用基类中方法的实现，或者覆盖一个或多个方法，来实现自己的版本。

他们还可以添加方法和属性，来扩展基类的功能。

让我们通过一个现实世界的例子来进一步探讨。

假设开发者创建了一个名为Coffee Maker的基类，它包含两个方法：water和beans。

首先，他们实现water方法，这样咖啡机对象就能在水箱中储水；实现beans方法，这样咖啡机就能接收咖啡粉。

然后，开发者创建了两个派生类，分别名为drip和super automatic，它们都扩展了Coffee Maker这个基类。

第一个派生类是drip类。

这个drip派生类完全照搬了基类中的方法，但增加了一个名为vessel的新方法，用来描述咖啡煮好后储存的容器。

第二个派生类是super automatic派生类。

super automatic派生类继承了water和beans这两个方法。

这个派生类完全照搬了基类中的water方法，因为这种型号的咖啡机也有一个水箱来储水。

但是，super automatic型号还能研磨咖啡豆，所以开发者覆盖了beans方法，将研磨功能包含进去。

接下来，在派生类中覆盖方法，能让开发者只修改派生类中需要变更的部分，而其他基类的功能则无需改动，直接使用。

我们来更详细地研究一下覆盖。

覆盖是面向对象编程中的一种技术，指的是派生类对已在基类中定义的方法，提供自己的实现。

这项技术允许开发者创建更具体类型的对象，并为方法定义它们自己的版本。

通过覆盖方法，开发者可以设计出能够改进或完全替换从基类继承的方法行为的派生类。

覆盖赋予了开发者一种能力，让他们能够在利用现有代码对象和特性的同时，设计出适合其特定上下文的派生类方法。

这鼓励了代码的重用，但又不以牺牲灵活性为代价。

在本视频中，你学习了如何解释面向对象编程中的多态性概念。

多态性赋予开发者强大的能力，可以在派生类中修改或扩展方法，使其根据作用的对象不同来执行不同的任务。

覆盖方法则让派生类能够提供它们自己关于方法功能实现的版本。

这些面向对象编程的概念帮助开发者编写出既灵活又可重用的代码。

If you upload photos,they may see a flipbook style interface that allows them to scroll through all the images.

The app uses a single interface for files,but it adapts to perform different actions depending on the type of file you upload.

Polymorphism enables developers to create a single method that performs different tasks based on the object they're acting on.

In this video,you'll learn how to explain the concept of polymorphism in object-oriented programming.

Let's begin by defining polymorphism.

Polymorphism enables developers to use a single method name to perform different tasks based on the type of object it's acting on.

This means a method can behave differently depending on what it's being asked to do.Here's an example of using polymorphism with an object.

Developers first create a derived class by extending a base class.

Through inheritance,the derived class has the methods and attributes of the base class.

At this point,they can then use the implementation of the methods in the base class or override one or more methods and implement their own version of them.

They can also add methods and attributes to extend the functionality of the base class.

First,they implement the water method so that the coffee maker object can hold water in a tank and the beans method so that the coffee maker accepts grounds.

The drip derived class uses the methods exactly as they are in the base class,but adds an additional method called vessel that describes where the coffee is stored after it's brewed.

The derived class uses the water method exactly as it is in the base class because this version of the coffee maker also has a tank to hold water.

However,the super automatic can also grind beans,so the developer overrides the beans method to include this functionality.

Next,overriding methods in derived classes enables developers to change only what they need in a derived class and use the other base class's functionality without modification.

Let's examine overriding in more detail.

Overriding is a technique in object-oriented programming where A derived class provides its own implementation of a method that is already defined in its base class.

This technique allows developers to create more specific types of objects that define their own version of a method.

By overriding a method,a developer can design derived classes that refine or completely replace the behavior of methods inherited from the base class.

Overriding gives developers the ability to design derived class methods that are appropriate to their specific context while using existing code objects and features.

This encourages code reuse,but not at the expense of flexibility.

Overriding a method lets derived classes provide their own versions of what a method can do.

03-03.01_在C#中实现继承

想象一下，拥有一个房屋的蓝图，它可以被重用和修改，用来建造不同类型的房屋。

编程中的继承类似地工作，它使用已有类中的属性和方法。

在本视频中，我们将在C#程序中应用继承。

通过在C#中使用继承，开发者可以创建一个新类，该类从现有类继承属性和方法。

现有的类称为基类，新类称为派生类。

首先，C#开发者通过使用访问修饰符来定义一个基类。

这些关键字决定了代码的其他部分是否可以访问这个类。

将一个类声明为public，意味着它的成员可以被任何其他类访问。

私有类成员只能在类本身内部访问，而受保护成员可以在该类内部以及派生类中访问。

在访问修饰符之后，开发者使用class关键字，后跟类的名称，来完成声明。

基类包含其他类可以继承的通用属性和方法。

属性保存数据，这些数据可能是类内部用来指导其如何运作的，也可能是该类将要处理的数据。

方法定义了该类可以执行的工作。

最后，基类有一个构造函数，它是类的入口点。

这就像房子的前门，你从这里进入，并声明你是谁以及你为什么在这里。

构造函数可以接受输入参数，开发者可以用这些参数来设置类属性的初始值。

让我们来看一个例子。

假设开发者想要创建一个管理泳池维护信息的类。

他们将这个类声明为public，它有两个属性，氯含量和水位。

构造函数将这些值作为输入参数，并将它们赋值给属性。

这个简单的类有一个名为泳池信息的方法，它会输出传入构造函数的值。

这个类拥有基类的所有要素，并且可以轻松地通过添加更多属性和方法进行扩展，使这个类变得更加强大。

开发者可以通过创建一个派生类来进一步扩展一个类的能力。

派生类继承了基类的所有属性和方法，开发者可以在不影响原始基类的情况下对其进行扩展。

让我们来看看这是如何工作的。

在C#中，派生类的定义方式与基类类似，通过使用一个访问修饰符和派生类的名称。

要创建一个派生类，开发者在名称后面加上一个冒号，并在声明中包含基类的名称。

派生类继承了基类的所有属性和方法。

开发者也可以添加自己新的属性和方法。

他们获得了基类的所有优点，但可以针对特定场景对其进行定制。

例如，开发者可以从泳池这个基类中创建一个温泉的派生类。

他们用public修饰符和类名spa来声明这个派生类，这个类派生自泳池这个基类。

这个派生类拥有基类所具有的氯含量和水位属性，但开发者决定添加一个名为热度的新属性，因为温泉是要加热的。

构造函数将所有三个属性的数据作为输入参数，并将它们赋值给派生类的属性。

这个派生类使用从基类继承来的泳池信息方法，在屏幕上显示温泉的信息。

总之，C#中的继承是一个强大的面向对象工具，它使开发者能够重用代码，并使C#代码更易于维护。

在本视频中，你学习了在C#程序中应用继承。

C#中的继承涉及创建一个基类，然后使用继承派生出一个派生类。

通过使用继承，开发者可以在C#中构建有组织、可重用且可维护的代码。

英文原文

Imagine having a blueprint for a house that can be reused and modified to build different types of houses.

Inheritance in programming works similarly using properties and methods from an already existing class.

In this video,we will apply inheritance in C#programs.

By using inheritance in C#,developers can create a new class that inherits properties and methods from an existing class.

The existing class is called the base class,and the new class is called the derived class.

To begin,C#developers define a base class by using an access modifier.

These keywords determine whether other parts of the code can access the class.

Declaring a class as public means its members are accessible from any other class.

Private class members are only accessible within the class itself,and protected members are accessible within the class and by derived classes.

After the access modifier,the developer uses the class keyword followed by the name of the class to finish the declaration.

The base class contains common properties and methods that other classes can inherit.

Properties hold data that the class will use internally to guide how it functions or data that the class will process.

The methods define the work the class can perform.

Finally,the base class has a constructor which is the entry point for the class.

It's like the front door of a house where you enter and declare who you are and why you're there.

The constructor can take input parameters which the developer can use to set the initial values for the class's properties.

Let's explore an example.

Suppose a developer wants to create a class that manages maintenance information for a pool.

They declare the class as public and it has two properties,chlorine level and water level.

The constructor takes these values as input parameters and assigns them to properties.

This simple class has a method called pool info that writes out the values that were passed into the constructor.

This class has all the elements of a base class and can be easily expanded with more properties and methods to make the class a lot more powerful.

Developers can further extend what a class can do by creating a derived class.

A derived class inherits all the properties and methods of the base class,and developers can extend it without impacting the original base class.

Let's examine how this works.

Derived classes are defined similarly to base classes in C#by using an access modifier and a name for the derived class.

To create a derived class,the developer follows the name by a colon and includes the name of the base class in the declaration.

The derived class inherits all the properties and methods of the base class.

Developers also can add their own new properties and methods.

They get all the advantages of the base class but can customize it for a particular scenario.

For example,a developer can create a derived class for a spa from the pool base class.

They declare the derived class with the public modifier and name of the class spa,which is derived from the pool base class.

The derived class has the chlorine level and water level properties that the base class has,but the developer decides to add a new property called heat level since spas are heated.

The constructor takes data for all three properties as input parameters and assigns them to the derived class's properties.

This derived class uses the pool info method inherited from the base class to display the spa's information on the screen.

In conclusion,inheritance in C#is a powerful object-oriented tool that enables developers to reuse code and make it easier to maintain C#code.
